# Delivery Racer: ideas checklist

Working list, from the owner's ideas of 2026-10-09. `[x]` done, `[~]` partly done (see the note),
`[ ]` not started, `[-]` built and then taken out. Each done item says what was and was not verified.

## State on 14-Oct (read with `src/delivery/HANDOVER-orchestrator-14-Oct.md`)

`main` is v1.1.133 (14-Oct, the owner's 'merge most of the session's branches': the cabinGlass helper and the Touring Car / Hill-Climb glass (274), the window audit (289), damage states in the model viewer (290), the ECU follow-up (291), the equippable number plate cosmetic (288), the hidden levels Haunted Hollow (280) with ghost traffic (292), Foliage Run (282) and Jungle Highway (283), the concept cars' wheel trims removed and the car agent prompt's window rules; items renumbered at the merge: plate cosmetic 286 -> 288, window audit 286 -> 289, viewer damage 286 -> 290, ECU follow-up 286 -> 291, ghost traffic 281 -> 292). Before that, `main` is v1.1.130 (14-Oct, 'Push everything that is done': ECU upgrades cost bank (285), the plate audit and plate tags (279), the Touring Car roof fix (274), and the hidden levels Chianti Run (281) and Dakar Dash (284). The hidden Halloween, Autumn and Rainforest levels and the plate cosmetic were stopped unfinished: their WIP is on claude/theme-halloween, claude/theme-autumn, claude/theme-rainforest, claude/plate-cosmetic, not for main). Before that, v1.1.129 (14-Oct, merged at the owner's "Merge into main" / "Push first": the ten idea cars of item 260 as items 263-267 (batches D1-D5, textures removed), nine more idea cars as items 270-274 (batches E1-E5; the Cinquecento was discarded), the global wheel colour 268, wheel designs 269, the Cosmetics equip screen 276 and the car catalogue 275; before that v1.1.128: true speed 257, the four damage stages v2 258 / 262, the handover docs). Waiting on the owner: 259 (cosmetics: wheels first, colour done as 268, designs next), 261 (level clocks after true speed). The rules for working here are in `CLOUD-AGENT-README.md`.

- [x] 86. Special events, merged in v1.1.9 (built on the feature branch `claude/special-events`; what was built and checked: `src/delivery/EVENTS-NOTES.md`). Checked before the merge, at the owner's word (rush it out): the tiers group with `.events-check`, the schema, tank and replay-store checks, `scripts/events.mjs check`; NOT the long groups (levels, replay determinism, smoke). Asked for (the owner, 11-Oct): within Events, a series of stages played in turn, each opening on its own date, all closing with the event; a required car (owned) or a free try (lent, as the special levels' cars are) per stage; a screen of stage cards with each stage's car in 3D at one pose (like Asphalt 9's car hunt); a limited car as the grand prize. Phases: rules and data, the screen, the prize car, a test event. Hold the merges.
- [ ] 77. Timed challenges (two players, or a daily challenge, 29) need FULLY SEEDED conditions (the owner, 11-Oct). Today the same two seeds (a replay's load and run seeds) give the same level and the same start, but not the same run: a mystery is rolled when it is picked up, from the one stream of dice the whole run shares (traffic, police and hazards draw from it too, as much as the driving makes them), and weighted by the player's social standing; so two players meet different mysteries. Needed: each mystery box (and other pickups' and hazards' chances, and if it is to be fully fair the traffic dealt out) drawing from dice of its own, from the seed and which box it is, so box 3 is the same for everyone whenever it is reached; no standing weighting in a challenge; a challenge code (level, side, car, both seeds) to share; then a friend's replay as the ghost. Changing how mysteries roll changes the rules (version.js logic): every replay and ghost recorded before it stops playing, so better done early.

Checked headless: sizes, a code out and back, five bad codes refused. Both panels seen in a screenshot (`?savepanel`, `?savepanel=import`); the Copy and Load buttons were not clicked, and the menu's refresh after an import was not seen.

- [ ] 115. IDEA ONLY, nothing built (the owner, 12-Oct): ways of getting cars. (a) Buy in the garage (as now). (b) Win from a level or special event (as now: `reward`, `prize`). (c) Blueprints: each car starts at 0/X blueprints and locked; X blueprints unlocks it, at ECU0; ECU0 cars are uncompetitive for their tier; each further X blueprints raises it one ECU; each ECU raises its stats; at max ECU it outperforms any garage car. Event cars get a magenta star tier (undecided whether that makes them wildcards). Open: where blueprints come from, what X is per car, how many ECUs, whether bought cars can be upgraded too, how tier rules (car locks, `allowedCars`, tier-money) treat a car whose stats change, and replays/ghosts (a car's fingerprint covers its figures, so an ECU change invalidates old replays and ghosts of it).
  - Item 115, owner's answers (12-Oct): blueprints come from a totally separate economy from the game's (money, tips); which one is undecided. The number of ECU levels varies car to car. Garage cars might have ECUs too: upgraded less, and even at their top still weaker than, or middling against, the rest of their tier (the event cars are the ones that overtake garage cars at max ECU).
  - Item 115, replays (owner, 12-Oct): the answer to ECU changes breaking replays is to record the car's stats as they were in the replay itself (so an ECU upgrade, or a rebalance, no longer invalidates it). To work out when built: record the figures the run used in the header (not only `carPrint`), play back with those; keep the car's id, model and perks checks; a ghost needs only the path, so it would not care.
  - Item 115, replays: DECIDE LATER (owner, 12-Oct). Recording only the player's car stats is not enough: everything in the run is driven by figures (traffic kinds, police, rivals, cargo, powerups, gimmicks), so a replay would have to keep the stats of everything in the level. And `cars.js` and the traffic tables are in the replay's `logic` hash (vite.config.js: every `src/delivery/*.js` not on NOT_RULES, with its imports), so any edit to them already refuses every replay, whatever the per-car fingerprint says (read, not tested). Two ways: (1) take the stat tables out of the hash, and have each recording carry a snapshot of every table it used, played back from the snapshot (old replays survive rebalances too; bigger header, bigger change); (2) keep the tables in the hash and record only a small per-car ECU modifier in the header (fixes upgrades, not rebalances; far smaller). Ghosts are unaffected either way (they read only the stored path).
  - Item 115, unlock kinds (owner, 12-Oct; this replaces the earlier "unlocked at ECU0"): ECU0 now means not driveable / locked. Two kinds of car. (1) KEY cars start at ECU1 and blueprints upgrade them, but they are not selectable (not unlocked) until their key is obtained; garage cars are key cars (the key is what they are bought or won with today). (2) BLUEPRINT cars start at ECU0 (locked) and are unlocked on reaching ECU1, so their first X blueprints are the unlock and the car then plays at ECU1. Open: what a key is for a won car (the reward/prize), whether blueprints collected for a key car before its key count, and what "uncompetitive for their tier" now means (it would be ECU1 of a blueprint car).
  - Item 115 (owner, 12-Oct): decided: a player CAN raise the ECU of a locked key car (blueprints count before the key is obtained); the car stays unselectable until the key, and then plays at whatever ECU it has reached.
  - Item 115, terminology (owner, 12-Oct), ideas only, nothing renamed in the game yet: the SHOWROOM is the new UI-based menu where cars are selected. The current garage stays as the place to buy the current cars, rebranded the CAR LOT, "Crazey Jim's Used Cars" (the owner's spelling). Tooltips on a car that cannot be driven yet: a key car (key not obtained): "You need the key to drive this car"; a garage car (not bought): "Purchase this car from the car lot". Note: "garage" is in the code, the menus, words, URLs (`?garage`, `garageTabBtn`...), CHECKLIST and the notes; a rename of the words players see is separate from renaming the code.
  - Item 115, card packs (owner, 12-Oct), ideas only: PACKS are the main source of blueprints, then special events. Some Career (main mode) levels offer a pack on first completion too. A pack holds cards of different RARITIES; a card is a blueprint for a car. COMMON (the bulk of a pack): blueprints for 1 to 4 star garage cars. UNCOMMON (in every pack): 5 star garage cars and all 1 to 4 blue star cars. EPIC: at least one card in every 10-pack is Epic or better; in a standard pack the Epics are all the 5 blue star cars plus a select few blueprint-only cars (the "evergreen" blueprint cars). LEGENDARY (above Epic): blueprint cars only. BESPOKE (the highest): the keys themselves are Bespoke cards. Open: pack sizes and kinds besides the standard and the 10-pack, odds per rarity, duplicates (a blueprint card for a car already at max ECU), what "blue star" and "magenta star" tiers each hold (tiers.json has stars today; the event cars were given magenta), how a Career first-completion pack differs from a bought one, and the separate economy that buys packs (undecided).
  - Item 115, pack kinds (owner, 12-Oct), ideas only: CAREER packs (first completion of some Career levels) contain only "evergreen" cars, which are the pack filler. SPECIAL packs have FEATURED cards (usually event cars): in each special pack the Epic-or-better slot is guaranteed to be a featured card, and every other slot has a chance to be upgraded; the featured cards drop at a 20% chance. Open (to read the 20% one way): is the 20% the chance, per other slot, that it upgrades to a featured card; or the chance that any given featured card comes up when a slot upgrades? And is the guaranteed featured slot still the pack's Epic-or-better one, with the 10-pack's one-Epic rule met by it?
  - Item 115, featured cards (owner, 12-Oct): the 20% is PER FEATURED CARD, rolled when a slot upgrades (so with several featured cards each has its own 20%). The LAST slot of a special pack is guaranteed a featured card, and it is also the pack's guaranteed Epic-or-better card (one slot doing both). Open: what a slot that upgrades but rolls no featured card becomes (a higher rarity, a different card), and whether several featured cards can come up at once from one slot.
  - Item 115 (owner, 12-Oct): every special pack has at least one featured card: a special pack is just a career pack (evergreen filler) with the featured-cards mechanic added. Each slot holds exactly one card, so one slot can never give two featured cards. (Read, not stated: a slot that upgrades but rolls no featured card stays its ordinary filler card. Say if not.)
  - Item 115 (owner, 12-Oct): a slot that upgrades but rolls no featured card upgrades to a higher-rarity FILLER (evergreen) card instead (this replaces my "stays ordinary" reading above).

Gold (normal) stars: 1 star: ECU1, no cards (never upgraded; the starter cars, maxed already); 2 star: ECU2, common; 3 star: ECU3, common; 4 star: ECU4, common; 5 star: ECU5, uncommon. Blue stars: 1 blue: ECU3, uncommon; 2 blue: ECU4, uncommon; 3 blue: ECU5, uncommon; 4 blue: ECU5, epic; 5 blue: ECU5, epic. So the ECU count follows the tier (1 to 5), and a car's maxed figures are today's. Differences from the earlier rarity note, for the owner to confirm: 1-star cars now have no cards (they had been common); 4 blue star cars are now epic (they had been uncommon with the 1 to 3 blue). Open: whether the number of blueprints for each ECU (X) also follows the tier, and how an ECU1 to ECU5 climb is shaped for a 5-star car whose ECU1 is far below its maxed figures.

  - Item 115, respawn time (owner, 12-Oct), plan only, `CONFIG.respawnTime` NOT changed yet (still 4.5 s for every car): base respawn time 5.5 s at ECU1, and 0.5 s less for each ECU above 1: ECU1 5.5, ECU2 5.0, ECU3 4.5, ECU4 4.0, ECU5 3.5 s. By the ladder above that makes a 1-star car (ECU1 only) 5.5 s, a 5-star 3.5 s, a 3-blue-star or better 3.5 s. Special cars (ECU1 cap) 5.5 s; starter cars (maxed, 1-star) 5.5 s. Fixed by adding `.shop-screen, #showroom, #packs` to it (v1.1.21). Cause read from the code; NOT tried on a real phone (the scroll was not reproduced in a browser either: headless Chromium has no touch scrolling to try here).

(b) The BLUEPRINT CARD (`render/blueprintcard.js`, `blueprintcard.css`): after Asphalt 9's, in the game's colours: rarity stripes (Common grey, Uncommon green, Epic purple, Legendary gold, Bespoke rainbow), the car in its pose, a black BLUEPRINT/KEY tab with a green x1, the RARITY LETTER (C, U, E, L, B: the card's rarity) in a black box at the lower left, the stars at the lower right, NEW, FEATURED, the name in two lines (`basedOn` for the filler/idea cars, else "Key car"/"Blueprint car"/"Event car", then the name in capitals). (c) The PACK REVEAL (`render/packs.js`, `packs.css`, replacing the Packs panel): a pack bought (Shop: after the confirm, no "bought" box) or earned (a Career level's first delivery, an event stage or prize: over the RESULTS, a beat after they show) opens at once full-screen in the Shop/Showroom look: the pack art shakes and bursts, the cards turn ON THEIR OWN one by one (a short beat, longer with a glow for Epic and above, the last with the most), the BEST card last (`packs.js revealOrder`: a special pack's featured card last unless a card upgraded ABOVE it), a tap anywhere turns all the rest at once, then Done / Next pack (n). Under each card its car's progress bar (ECU pips, n/X, MAX) fills from before that card to after it (`packs.js revealProgress`, in reveal order), and GLOWS with "UNLOCKED!" / "ECU n!" on an unlock or ECU up. A pack not yet opened stays in `Progress.data.packs` (pending) and opens at the next menu; `Packs.next()` opens one at a time (no double open). (d) "What's inside" uses compact blueprint cards (3 a row on a phone); the Shop's featured car and the showroom cards use the poses. Every pack-screen picture is drawn in the GOOD livery. BUILT: (a) ecus.json: every ECU of every car now also has `agility` (cars.js `agility`, steering), `delivery` (package efficacy, new), `cool` (the shoulder meter's fuse, new; the name COOL: 'cool' is otherwise only gunfire.js's private cooldown map, no stat or word), `crossing` (cars.js `crossing`: rails, fords, mud, the tide) and `weight` (cars.js `mass`); the first four by the same equal steps as the others (f 0.03 a car lot car, 0.06 a blueprint/event/Bespoke car; to 0.001 so each ECU is above the last), weight the same at every ECU; the maxed ECU = the car's own figure (agility, crossing, mass as today; cool 1.0 for every car). Written by `node scripts/ecu-tables.mjs --stats` (adds the fields a car's ECUs lack, by the file's own ECU count, keeping every hand edit; idempotent), `--missing` (new cars, with every field), `--delivery` (rewrites only `delivery`, from the table); `ecu.js` `NEW_STATS` says what each is at the max, `Ecu.stats(car, ecu)` returns them (an entry written before them: the car's maxed figures). (b) DELIVERY: each car's own figure at its max ECU, cooked up, in `ecu.js` `DELIVERY` (edit there, or a car's `delivery` field in cars.js, then `--delivery`), 0.6 to 1.6 with a reason per group: mail/courier vehicles best (Post Van and Courier Special 1.6, Milk Float 1.55, Kei Truck and Float Van 1.5, Road 8x8 1.45), vans 1.3-1.35, trucks and working machines 1.1-1.3, estates/SUVs 1.0-1.2, small cars 0.9-1.0, style cars 0.8-0.85, sports cars and the Junker 0.65-0.75, novelties, record cars and racers 0.6. What it scales (packages.js `deliver`): an Evil flaming package's damage (and so its spin-out and the driver's anger) and a Good gift's standing gain (`Social.gift(cop, efficacy)`); not a gift's 4-point scratch, moods, busts, Big Splash's reach or pelting. - [x] 137. The showroom landing looks SPRUNG (the owner, 12-Oct: "get the bounce to actually dampen the wheels on every car... it looks like there's suspension. Every car looks stiff right now"). Branch `worktree-agent-a2c3088c722b5d7e5`, not merged, no version bump. BUILT: `render/carsuspension.js` (`prepareSuspension(model)`, worked out once per model and kept on `userData.suspension`, and `applySuspension(model, body, o)`, used by `render/showroomview.js`, so it covers both the desktop full view and the phone's single-car screen). It finds each model's tyres by SHAPE however the model was built: a disc on its side that touches the floor (the shared `wheel()` helpers, a model's own CylinderGeometry, a torus), plus what sits inside its round (hub, spokes, whitewall, tread), and a flat decal on the floor (an underglow). Those are held on the floor while the body sinks onto them and rises off them, with a touch of pitch and roll, so the arches close and open round the tyres. The wheels fall with the car until it lands (`carbounce.js` now gives `wheelY`). A long car pitches less. A low body is eased off short of the floor (a soft bump stop: the Toybota and Float Van compress ~1.5-1.8 cm instead of 3.4). Any other part of a wheeled car that stands on the floor (the camper's awning poles) is stretched upright. The amplitude, timing (0.8 s), reduced motion (no motion) and silhouettes (fade-in only) are unchanged. NO BOUNCE (the owner: some vehicles need none): a vehicle with no wheels on the floor, or a model with `userData.noBounce`, only fades in: the Monowheel (`noBounce`: its carriage hangs from rollers on the hoop), the Saucer, the Jetboat, and the hovercraft and snowcat ideas. COVERAGE: 116 cars (the showroom's roster, which includes the 18 fillers, the 6 Bespoke key cars, the event and earned cars, plus every car idea). 110 have their wheels found by shape with no model touched, 1 also has stretched poles (the camper), and 5 do not bounce. `render/showcar.js` (`makeShowCar`, `paintShowCar`, moved out of garage.js, which re-exports them) lets a check build the models without the car lot. `?showroom&look=<id>&full&bounce=0.16` holds the landing at 0.16 s (the compression peak; 0.31 is the rebound) without the fade or the turning, for a picture. The words, render, tiers and cars groups passed (`--no-lock`; render and tiers again after the no-bounce change). - [ ] 167. ADDING A CAR MUST NOT INVALIDATE EVERY REPLAY (the owner, 12-Oct: "invalidating all replays because I added something is really harsh"): `ecus.json` is in the replay `logic` hash (vite.config.js: a file is in the hash unless it is on NOT_RULES), so adding one car's ECU table (item 166 added the Goddess Saloon's and the Fifties Hardtop's) changes the hash and every saved replay is refused. TO DO: stop hashing the whole file. Options, simplest first: (a) a replay records the ECU table entry it ran (its car's stats at its ECU) in its header and plays back from THAT, so the file need not be hashed at all (the replay already records the ECU level); (b) hash only each car's own entry and check the replay's car's entry, so adding or editing another car never refuses it; (c) at least leave added entries out of the hash and hash only the entries that existed. Also check which other data a new car touches that is hashed (cars.js stats are; a new car entry should not change an existing car's replays), and add a check: add a car to the tables and an old replay still plays. Not done; the Goddess/Hardtop promotion (166) refuses old replays until this is fixed.

- [x] 183. Back from the Shop and the showroom goes to the menu cleanly (the owner, 13-Oct: on a phone, back did not return cleanly). What was wrong: `nav.js` read back by the depth number in `history.state` alone, and that state outlives the page. After a reload, or a phone that dropped the tab and loaded it again, with a screen open, the old entries were still there with their numbers: the next screen's entry came after them, so back landed on an old entry of the same or a higher number and closed nothing (the Shop or the showroom stayed up), and back from the menu took one or two dead presses before leaving the site. A forward step onto the entry of a screen closed by its own Back did nothing either. Also, the Shop opened from the showroom set `Game.inMenu` false on closing while the showroom was still up (Enter could start a run from it). Fixed: every entry carries its depth AND this load's mark; the menu's entry is taken over when the page loads; back closes the screens above the depth landed on; an earlier load's entries are stepped over, a forward step onto a closed screen is stepped back (`nav.js`); the Shop gives back the `Game.inMenu` it found (`render/shop.js`). New check `scripts/.nav-check.mjs` (a stand-in history: nested screens, own Back buttons, two closed at once, forward steps, reloads, a second round; 24 of its lines fail on the old `nav.js`), in the tiers group. Checked in headless Chromium at 390x844, touch, mobile, before (main) and after, each sequence twice in a row then one more back: menu > Shop > Back / back; menu > Showroom > Back / back; Showroom > a car's full view > Back > Back / back x2; Showroom > its Shop > Back > Back / back x2; full view > Shop > Back x3 / back x3; Showroom > Crazey Jim's > Back > Back / back x2; the filters sheet > Done > Back / back x2; a reload on the full view then the Shop and back; a reload on the Shop then the showroom and back. Before: 14 of 16 (both reload cases left the Shop or the showroom up); after: 16 of 16, the menu each time, the extra back leaving the site, no console errors; the menu's stills read. Also `.showroom-check`, `.shop-check`, `.packs-check`, the build. NOT checked: a real phone (its own back gesture, iOS Safari's swipe, a tab dropped and loaded again by the phone); in headless Chromium the plain sequences already worked before the fix, so if the owner saw the bug without a reload or a dropped tab, it was not reproduced here.

- [x] 212. WHAT'S INSIDE ON A HIGH-DPR TABLET (the owner's Xiaomi Pad 7: Android Chrome, desktop site, touch, device pixel ratio about 3, 144 Hz; flicker while scrolling and while still, cards flashing, poor performance). Branch `claude/mipad-inside`, NOT merged, no version bump (placeholder number: renumber at merge if 212 is taken). FOUND (headless Chromium through the DevTools protocol, desktop UA, touch emulation, which reports `pointer: coarse` like the tablet; 1280x850 and 1067x712 CSS px at ratio 1 and 3): NOT the WebGL side. No card has a WebGL context (one shared renderer), no context was lost, each still is capped at ratio 2 (367x275 px), 61 encoded for the standard pack's 66 cards, and every card ends as a background picture with no live canvas. The cause is a COMPOSITING-LAYER EXPLOSION: the shine's `mix-blend-mode: screen` (Epic, Legendary and Bespoke cards' `.bp-art::before`) blends with whatever lies behind the art, so Chrome cannot merge those cards into the scroller's layer. It gives them, and the cards painted after them, layers of their own. Standard pack: 136 composited layers (64 of them "Overlap"), 209 Mpx of layer area at ratio 3 (17 Mpx at ratio 1), 68 MB of GPU tiles once scrolled. Car Hunt pack at 1067x712: 105 layers. FIX: `isolation: isolate` on `.bp-art` (blueprintcard.css), so the blend stays inside the art, which is opaque: the same pixels. Standard: 136 to 85 layers, tiles 68 to 41 MB scrolled; Car Hunt: 105 to 32 layers, tiles 62 to 34 MB. Look: screenshots before and after at ratios 1, 2 and 3, scrolled to three places with animations frozen, differ only where a car's lazily drawn picture had or had not arrived yet (and the menu scene behind); the cards' styling is the same. Checks: .touch-check, .nav-check, .extras-hit-check, .packs-check and .shop-check pass (0 FAIL), vite build passes; eslint has nothing to check (a CSS change only), and its 13 errors in src/delivery were already on main. NOT verified: on a real Mi Pad 7 or any Android device (the tile-memory budget and GPU there cannot be emulated, and headless frame times under SwiftShader mean nothing), so whether this ends the flicker is unproven. Still standing: the Bespoke and Legendary-key cards' compositor animations (bp-glow-rainbow and bp-glow-gold animate `filter` on the whole card, bp-twinkle animates opacity; 21 layers and their overlaps), and the menu's 3D scene, which keeps drawing under the sheet (about 60 WebGL draw calls a frame) together with the menu's stage-drift layer (1792x1190 CSS px). These are the next suspects if the tablet still flickers. RESULT ON THE REAL TABLET (the owner, v1.1.98): "even worse if anything"; the `isolation: isolate` line is REVERTED in item 213 (headless layer counts proved nothing about the device).

- [x] 213. The car on the BIG pack of a pack opening was cut off on a larger screen (the owner, 13-Oct: "The pack art for the Silver Comet is cut off ... on a larger screen ... it's the big pack"). Branch `claude/comet-bigpack-fix`, NOT merged, no version bump. What was wrong, three things in how a pack's car still (render/packart.js packCar, carstill.js, modelviews.js drawStill) was drawn and shown: (1) `.shop-pack .pack-car` gave its background NO size, so the picture showed at its pixel size, and a still is drawn at the device pixel ratio: on a 2x screen (Retina, an iPad, most large high-DPI screens) a picture twice the box, only its top left quarter (the nose) seen; (2) the still was sized and keyed by `getBoundingClientRect`, the box ON SCREEN, which the big pack's shake (packs.css pr-shake: scale .6 to 1.7, turned) makes another size and shape than the span: drawn late in the shake (scaled up) it was bigger than the box, cut off even at 1x, and framed for the wrong shape; (3) it was queued AFTER the reveal's ten card stills (packs.js queues them first, the pack's car came a frame later), so on a large screen (five cards a row, bigger stills, more to draw) it was drawn late in the shake or not at all before the pack burst (in headless Chromium at 1280 wide and up, main's pack had no car at all during the shake). Fixed: modelviews.js `boxOf(el)` (the element's own laid-out size, offsetWidth/Height, no transforms) sizes drawStill and carstill.js keyOf; shop.css `.pack-car` is `center / contain no-repeat`, and carstill.js show() gives `contain` to any element whose CSS gives the background no size; drawSoon takes `first` (drawn at once if laid out, else first in the queue; one queued job an element); packCar queues its car first when made and redraws it when its size changes (a ResizeObserver, a step of 20 px). Check: `scripts/.packcar-check.mjs` (new, in the tiers group): boxOf against a shaken box, drawStill's canvas at 2x the span's own size, every car any pack features (the Silver Comet, the Blower) framed whole by PACK_POSE at the big pack's shape and five others (1.4 to 3 : 1; the turned box reaches 0.90 of the edge, the pose's margin), the contain CSS, boxOf in keyOf and drawStill, first + ResizeObserver; it fails on main (no boxOf). Checked: node --check, eslint, `.packcar`, `.packs`, `.shop`, `.carsources`, `.showroom`, `.extras-hit`, `.modelviews` checks, vite build. SEEN in headless Chromium (swiftshader), the big pack held still (animations off) at 1280x800, 1920x1080, 2560x1440, 1366x1024, 2732x2048 and 390x844, each at DPR 1 and 2, `?pack=comet-special` and `?pack=car-hunt-5`: before, at 390x844@2 the Comet (and the Blower) was its nose only, cut at the pack's right and bottom (whole at @1); at the larger sizes main drew no car during the pack stage at all; after, the whole car, centred, at every size and DPR, sharp at 2x. Timing (a trace): the pack's car is now on the first frame after the reveal opens (before the cards). The SAME cut-off was on every small pack too: the Car Hunt event card's pack (`?event=car-hunt`) at 1280x800, 1920x1080, 1366x1024 and 2732x2048 @2 showed only the Blower's nose before (@1 whole) and the whole car after; the Shop's tiles (their packs are turned, so the old box on screen gave the still the wrong shape: 246x143 for a 236x120 span at 2x) are whole after at every size. NOT verified: a real large screen or tablet (the owner's), a real GPU's timing (swiftshader frames take ~0.8 s here, so the cut-off as the owner saw it, a still drawn mid-shake on a real GPU, was reasoned from the code, not reproduced at 1x); a window resized during the shake.

- [x] 217. SHOWROOM: A TAP DURING A PAN RESTARTED IT (the owner, 13-Oct, on a tablet: "On the showroom, tapping left or right while the screen pans resets the animation and becomes really jarring"). Branch `claude/showroom-pan`, NOT merged, no version bump (placeholder number: renumber at merge if 217 is taken). CAUSE: the row's ‹ › arrows (and the arrow keys sideways) used the browser's smooth scroll (`row.scrollBy` / `scrollIntoView`, behavior smooth): each tap began a NEW smooth scroll from where the row was, from standing, with the row's proximity snap pulling in between; the row stood still for frames, started again, and taps were lost. FIX: the pan is ours (src/delivery/render/showroompan.js, pure; driven per frame by render/showroom.js): one tap is the same ease-in-out slide as before (smoothstep, 380 ms, onto a column's start: the same end as before for one tap); a tap during a pan moves the TARGET on by a step from the target (not from where the row is) and the slide carries on from where it is at the speed it has (a cubic Hermite, the speed held so it never passes the target and comes back); a tap the other way turns it round smoothly; the snap is off while it moves (class `panning`) and it ends on a column start so nothing is pulled after; a finger, the mouse or the wheel on the row takes over. The arrow keys (sideways rows) go through the same pan; the phone's column and the phone's single-car slide are unchanged. CHECK: scripts/.showroom-check.mjs `rowPan`: on a fake 60 fps clock, 2 / 4 / 8 taps 70 ms apart never move back, never stand still before the end, never return to the start offset, and end exactly N column steps on; one tap is eased in and out; a reversal turns round with no jumping frame; the ends are kept; the old restart-from-standing model stalls (so the check sees it). SEEN (headless Chromium 1194 through the DevTools protocol, 1280x800, sampling `scrollLeft` every frame): 4 taps 120 ms apart, before: ended at 1786 of about 4080 (taps lost), 2 frames standing still mid-pan; after: 4076, 0 standing, 0 px backwards. 6 taps 70 ms apart, before 1531 (2 standing), after 6112 (0). One tap: 1022 before and after. Max backwards jump per frame was 0 px before and after in headless Chromium (it did not show the snap BACK the owner saw; that is likely the tablet browser's snap/smooth scroll). 8 quick ArrowRight presses: a smooth pan, 0 back, 0 standing. NOT verified: a real tablet (touch on the Xiaomi Pad, its 144 Hz), Safari/iPadOS, how the pan looks to the eye (no stills of motion read), the phone's vertical single-car slide (a CSS animation per car, not this pan; it still restarts per swipe). Checks run: .showroom-check, .touch-check, .nav-check, .extras-hit-check, eslint on the changed files, vite build.

- [x] 234. A CAR MODEL EDITOR (the owner, 13-Oct: "I would like a model editor, I inspect each car, look at what is drawn and manipulate the parts. Level editor but for car JSONs"). Branch `claude/model-editor`, NOT merged, no version bump (placeholder number: renumber at merge if 230 is taken). The models stay CODE; an edit is a PATCH over what the code builds, so the code's hooks (wheels, lamps, livery, animate, suspension) keep working. PAGE `delivery/modeleditor.html` (src/delivery/modeleditor.js, modeleditor.css over the level editor's editor.css): pick any roster car or car idea; a 3D view (orbit / pan / zoom by mouse or one / two fingers, Front / Rear / Left / Right / Top / 3/4, a grid and a soft footprint shadow, Good / Evil, Wire, Bounds, the cars.js Hitbox, the Wheels' centres); the PARTS list (every mesh and group: its name, the code's hook (userData.body, wheels...) or a guessed role: tyre, hub, glass, headlamp, tail lamp, indicator, bumper, mirror, body, part; its kind and triangles; search; eye, solo, lock: this view only); a tap or click in the view selects (raycast; the mouse over a part shows its box), the selected part has a box and a GIZMO (Move G / Rotate R / Scale S, Snap); the PART tab: name, role, position / rotation (degrees) / scale, the sizes of a plain box / cylinder / sphere / cone / torus / plane / circle (when the code did not move it inside its geometry), colour, glow, opacity, "colour every part sharing its material" (one material copy: no extra draw call), "paint for Evil only", shown; Add box / cylinder / sphere / cone, Duplicate (Ctrl+D), Mirror (M: a copy across the car that follows its part), Delete, Reset part; Undo / Redo (Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y, a stack of patches); the JSON tab (the live patch, typed edits applied when valid); Download / Load (or drop) a patch; autosave per car in localStorage; a STATS strip against CAR-AGENT-PROMPT.md's budget (shapes, triangles, draw calls once merged, edits, stale edits). The page draws with the game's own renderer (render/scene.js on its `<canvas id="game">`: one WebGL context, pixels capped at 2x / 1.5x on touch); render/modeleditorshim.js stands in for the game page's elements that game.js and others grab as they load. THE PATCH (render/modelpatch.js, pure: extractParts, applyPatch, withSet / withDelete / withAdd / withDuplicate / withMirror): `{ car, version, base, ops: [{id, sig, set}|{id, sig, delete}|{add: {id, kind, params, ...}|{id, from, mirror}}], evil: [{id, sig, set}] }`; ids are child-index paths resolved on the model AS BUILT before any change, a sig (geometry type, vertex count, size; not the place) re-finds a part the code moved; an id that finds nothing is reported as unmatched and skipped (never thrown); a different `base` warns that the code changed. Colours clone the material (never one a traffic copy shares); the paint is re-applied after the model's own livery (userData.livery wrapped). THE GAME (render/modelpatchapply.js): `src/delivery/modelpatches/<car id>.json`, loaded lazily (import.meta.glob dynamic imports: none today, the game bundle grew 0.3 kB), applied ONCE per model build where every car model is made: render/showcar.js makeShowCar (garage, inspector, showroom, stills, menu), render/items.js playerModel (the car driven, cached per car), render/ideaslot.js; `?modelpatch=local` uses the editor's patches from this browser, `?modelpatch=off` none. LINKS: Options > Guide "Model editor" next to "Level editor" (both 44 px tall on a touch screen), the level editor's header, the garage inspector's "Edit model" (opens the editor on that car and side in a new tab). TOOLING: `scripts/model-patch.mjs <car> --dump | --check [file] | --apply [file]` and `--all --check`; `scripts/.modelpatch-check.mjs` (in the render group). Z-FIGHTING FROM THE GEOMETRY (the owner, 13-Oct: "Is there a procedural way you can detect all z fighting instances in all the models"): `scripts/.zfight-check.mjs` builds every car headless, takes every visible triangle into world space, finds coplanar overlapping pairs (planes within 4 mm) and keeps only those a viewer's ray meets together from outside (honouring material sides, nothing in front, never from underneath). Writes a report (`--report=<md>`); exit code 1 while any are found, so it is NOT in a test group yet. First (corrected) run: 270 pairs in 66 cars: 82 will flicker (under 0.5 mm apart), 110 might (0.5-4 mm), 78 are doubled same-colour surfaces (no flicker). The detector is `render/zfight.js`, also used by the model editor's Z-fight tab (scan, click a row to see both surfaces, Lift A/B to stand 5 mm in front, Delete A/B). THE OWNER DECIDES which surface wins for each; then fix, then add the script to the render group.

- [x] 257. TRUE SPEED (the owner, 14-Oct: "Lets implement true speed" = real lane lengths on bends; then "Lane changes should scrub some speed as well... part of the agility stat? It still wouldn't be very significant"). Branch `claude/charming-maxwell-pxoail`, NOT merged, no version bump. `CONFIG.trueSpeed` { on, least 0.4, most 2.5, laneScrub 1.2 }; `Track.pathScale(s, lat)` = 1 / (1 - bend x lat), so progress along the road is the distance driven over the length of the car's own line (inside of a bend shorter, outside longer). Applied to the player (`this.s += speed*dt*pathScale`) and to ordinary traffic / racers (traffic.js main advance). Lane changes scrub `laneScrub x across^2 / agility` m/s^2 (across = sideways speed over the car's full sideways speed), never below minSpeed: under 1 m/s for a lane change at 40 m/s, and a nimble car loses less. NOT changed: the screensaver dolly, special traffic states (flyovers, ramps, pursuit), the lap/finish maths (they use s, so a tight inside line is simply faster). REPLAYS: the rules fingerprint changes (player.js / track.js / config.js / traffic.js are inside it), old replays and ghosts are refused (accepted by the owner). CLEAN-RUN TIMES (scripts/level-clocks.mjs --all, flag on vs off; the run keeps the centre of its lane): 34 of 44 timed levels within 0.5%; circuits slower by 3.5-10.6% (grand-prix 222.8 -> 230.6, spa 198.5 -> 207.4, marina-bay 216.9 -> 229.1, montreal 190.1 -> 201.8, stelvio 179.8 -> 191.2, mystery-meadows 98.0 -> 108.4): the clean run drives the lane centre, which on a mostly outside-heavy line is longer. The written level clocks / par / medal times are NOT re-written yet (a decision for the owner). CHECKED: `scripts/.truespeed-check.mjs` (new), level-clocks both ways. NOT verified: driving it in a browser (how it feels), rivals' races, ghost racing across lanes, the tiers group after the change.

- [~] 258. FOUR HAND-MADE DAMAGE STAGES (the owner, 14-Oct: "Instead of procedurally generating damage, we should just define 4 different levels of damage"; then "This dent shape idea needs an audit too. Many dents currently look awful", "Make a sampling first", "Go with the ideas and retry the same vehicles"). Branch `claude/charming-maxwell-pxoail` has the first cut (commit 9cded11, NOT merged, no version bump): `render/dents.js` DAMAGE = 4 stages of fixed dents laid on the whole car in car space (across / height / length), `CONFIG.dents.steps` [0.2, 0.4, 0.6, 0.8], `scripts/.dents-check.mjs` updated and passing. SAMPLED (8 cars, stills from a headless scratch viewer, not the game camera): far better than the old random shove (spiky shards from level 1), but lamps / grilles / stripes / mirrors float off the moved paint, glass shows through roofs and sides, front wings fold into wheels, low-poly cars (sport, F1, bus, darkvan, tuk-tuk) show nothing at stages 1-2, the dents are too big on the bus. Verdicts: commuter good-ok, darkvan ok, junker ok (headlamps float), F1 ok-weak, sport ok-bad, tuk-tuk ok-bad, lowrider bad (pinstripe), bus bad. FIXES IN PROGRESS on branch `claude/damage-audit` (agent): attached parts follow the dents, glass / wheel clamps, one-off subdivision of low-poly panels, a dent size cap in metres, stronger stages 1-2. NOT verified: the wider 30-car audit, other views, Evil liveries, night, the game's own camera, dents in a real level, a phone. UPDATE: the v2 rework (item 262, merged into this branch from `claude/damage-audit`, NOT on main) fixed these: 6 of 8 sampled cars good (commuter, sport, junker, lowrider good; darkvan ok, F1 ok, tuk-tuk ok, bus ok but too subtle). WATCH: a damaged player car is 1.5-6x the triangles (commuter 1,764 -> 8,288, junker 2,146 -> 13,158, darkvan 10,689, lowrider 11,762) because low-poly panels are subdivided; the owner should decide if that is acceptable on the Mi Pad.
- [ ] 259. EQUIPPABLE COSMETICS FOR EVERY CAR, AUDITED (the owner, 14-Oct: wheels, licence plate and roof ornaments at most, other ideas welcome). The audit agent measured 144 models + 40 Super versions (geometry and ray casts only, nothing seen in a browser). Reasonable on every car: paint per side, finish, wheel / hub colour, underglow colour, horn, headlamp tint, boost-flame colour. - [x] 262. DAMAGE LOOK AUDITED AND REWORKED (the owner, 14-Oct: "many dents currently look awful"; then, after an 8-car sample, "Go with the ideas and retry the same vehicles"). Branch `claude/damage-audit` (from 9cded11), NOT merged, no version bump. Render-side only (`src/delivery/render/dents.js`, `scripts/.dents-check.mjs`; config and the rules files are untouched). The four stages stay; what changed: (1) parts that are not paint now follow the dents too: small ones (lamps, grilles, mirrors, under 0.35 m) are moved whole by the dents at their centre, larger ones (bumpers, stripes, pinstripes, a roof or cab in a paint of its own, a tarp, a canopy) are bent like the panels. Glass (found by its colour), the wheels and what turns with them (found by render/carsuspension.js), see-through and instanced meshes, and the lamp effects are left alone. (2) The dents are held back: near the glass the paint does not move, so it never goes behind it; nothing gets nearer than 0.6 of its distance to a wheel or the glass; a shove toward the middle stops 0.45 of the way there. The table is now gentle enough that the dents never fold the car through itself (checked numerically: the dent field's Jacobian stays positive). (3) Panels are cut finer once per model, only where the dents bend them (an edge is halved where the dents at its middle differ from the mean of its ends by more than 2 cm, or 5 mm where paint and another part lie together). The cut is kept, and is made on a timer when the car is first seen, not on the first knock. (4) A dent's reach and depth are capped at a big car's size in metres (1.0 / 1.6 / 2.4 m), so a bus is dented at its corners, not along half its length. (5) Stages 1 and 2 were re-authored (a front corner, the tail and a door, then a creased bonnet and a caved door), and the whole table rescaled. Bug fixed on the way: the crumpled copies were computed in the frame the car stood in when first measured, so a car that had driven on was dented in the wrong place. Triangles (the player's car only, at any stage; clean is unchanged): commuter 1764 to 8288, sportscompact 1000 to 10582, junker 2146 to 13158, darkvan 1236 to 10689, city bus 1020 to 2542, lowrider 1984 to 11762, F1 676 to 1524, tuk-tuk 1112 to 3922. Measuring and cutting takes 50 to 300 ms in Node, once per model.
  - CHECKED: `.dents-check` (rewritten: glass and wheels untouched, a low-poly panel cut finer, corners together, a lamp moved whole, a stripe bent and not swallowed, nothing past the middle or into a wheel, clean again, the same dents after the car has moved and turned); eslint on both files; `--group render --no-lock` (10 of 10 pass); vite build. LOOKED at (headless Chromium/swiftshader contact sheets, studio light, clean and stages 1 to 4, front and rear three-quarters) on the commuter, sportscompact, junker, darkvan, city bus, lowrider, F1 and tuk-tuk, compared with the first version and the old procedural damage.

A design = tyre treatment + hub treatment, all rings / discs / bands (rotationally symmetric: reads the same still or turning; no spokes). Six presets: Stock; Whitewall (white ring, 0.64 to 0.88 of the tyre radius, on the outer sidewall); Redline (thin red ring, 0.78 to 0.84); Red tyres, white hubs (rubber recoloured red, hubs white); White hubs on black; Gold hubs, whitewall. Per wheel: a ring is one RingGeometry (16 segments = 32 triangles; one shared geometry per ring shape, one shared material per colour, both flagged shared), 5 mm off the tyre's outer face, a child of the tyre mesh so it rides with the planted wheel; a centre wheel (trike front) gets one each side (64). Colour-only designs add no triangles. Rubber recolour (tyre, tread blocks, sidewall rings) uses per-model clones, never the shared cached materials. Interaction with 268: the hub colour row still works and wins: a hub colour other than Stock overrides the design's hubs (tyres keep the design); hub colour Stock keeps the design's own hubs (or the car's). Hub clones also get a little emissive (30%) so white reads white in shade (this lightly brightens the 268 colours too). Only tyres that have a hub of their own get treated; a car with no hub found stays stock (Tank; no-tyre cars; Legendary stay stock by design). Kept in localStorage `delivery.wheeldesign` (try/catch, ignored and unsaved under Progress.noSave); `?wheeldesign=<id>` for stills. BUILT, barebones: a car now has a REACHED ECU (`Ecu.reached`: its blueprints, X per step, capped by the ladder, exactly as before) and a PURCHASED one (`Ecu.purchased`, saved in `Progress.data.ecuBought = { [carId]: ECU }`, only cars above their free first ECU have an entry); it PLAYS at the purchased one (`Ecu.level`). Rule chosen for blueprint cars (a QUESTION for the owner): ECU1 stays FREE (a key car: its key; a blueprint car: its first X blueprints unlock it, as ever, it is "owned" by them), every ECU above is a purchase from the bank (`Progress.data.money`, never Crazey Cash), one step at a time, in order, only up to the reached ECU and only with the key (`Ecu.upgrade(car)` says why not: none / maxed / nokey / locked / blueprints (needs N more) / short (needs $N more) / ready; `Ecu.buy(car)`). PRICES: `CONFIG.ecuCost` in config.js (PLACEHOLDERS for the balancing pass): step to ECU n = ref(stars) x 0.2 x 1.5^(n-2), rounded to $5, ref = a typical car lot price of the stars (gold 50/170/310/450/700, blue 290/450/730/1000/1270, no stars 1500), `perCar` overrides; maxing costs 0.16 to 1.8 x the car's own lot price (a 2-ECU car $35, a gold 5 $1,140 with a last step of $475, a blue 5 $2,060, an event/Bespoke car $2,440); the table: `node scripts/.ecu-cost-check.mjs --table`. UI: the showroom's full view (render/ecuupgrade.js + ecuupgrade.css) shows, for a car whose next ECU is in sight, an "Upgrade to ECU3: $1,200" button (green when it can be bought; dimmed with "Need $N more in the bank" or "Needs N more blueprints to unlock ECU3" when not; 44 px, keyboard, `aria-describedby` the reason) and the figures the step changes (ecus.json, unchanged); buying redraws the showroom at once (bank, pips, live car, figures), no reload. The pips show bought (filled) and unlocked-not-bought (outlined). MIGRATION: a save with no `ecuBought` reads as null and `Ecu.migrate()` (run when ecu.js loads, before blueprints are added, and before a save is written) fills it from the blueprint levels (every car keeps the ECU it has: no refund, no charge; a car in `maxed` needs no entry), once, idempotent; `maxed` means what it meant. "Unlock everything" (both modes): every car it deals is bought up to its max, a blueprint car blueprints had unlocked up to what they reached; mode abs otherwise unchanged. Address switches: `&bp=` (showroom) now only gives blueprints (it unlocks an upgrade); NEW `&ecu=midnight:1,sport:2` sets purchased levels for the visit, `&money=` the bank; `&owns=` still maxes the car (as before). COOKIE: `ecuBought` follows the blueprints (in the cookie, dropped with them and `maxed` if the cookie is too long, so the 4096 cap holds): a fresh save +27 bytes, every one of the 36 lot cars fully bought +714 bytes (measured by `.ecu-cost-check`, which reads document.cookie in the headless page); local storage has the whole save. Free tries (`ownEcu` events, events.js / ecu.js `drive`) still play at the REACHED ECU (a key car without its key cannot be bought up, and the Concept Strike key needs the max ECU). - [x] 291. ECU FOLLOW-UP, THE OWNER'S DECISIONS OF 14-OCT (A to F; branch `claude/ecu-followup`, from main v1.1.130, NOT merged, version not bumped). BUILT, barebones then polish. A. KEYLESS KEY CARS MAY BUY: `Ecu.upgrade` has no `nokey` state any more (a key car without its key is `ready` / `short` / `blueprints` like any other; `locked` is the blueprint car's alone); `Ecu.buy` works, the car's `level()` stays 0 (unselectable, "Purchase this car from the car lot" / "You need the key...") until the key, then it plays at the level BOUGHT. THE KEY CONDITION (the Concept Strike's 'deliver at its max ECU'): events.js `meets` still reads `run.ecu >= Ecu.max(car)`, and `run.ecu` is the ECU the run was DRIVEN at (`carStats(CAR).ecu`), which is now always the BOUGHT level; so 'max ECU' = the bought level: the Fishbowl's key needs 20 blueprints AND ECU2..5 paid for. THE FREE-TRY EXCEPTION IS DROPPED: `Ecu.drive` for a free try (`level.ownEcu`) now plays at `Ecu.purchased(car)` (was `Ecu.reached`), key or none; the card chip (`Events.freeTry().ecu`), the menu's 'Free try - ECU n/m' and the try button's tooltip read the bought level too; nothing broke (.events-check updated: blueprints alone drive at ECU1, 20 blueprints + ECU3 bought deliver at ECU3 and give NO key, ECU5 bought gives the key). Upgrade block: shown for a keyless key car with the note 'You can upgrade it now; you still need its key to drive it' (not for a blacked-out card). "Unlock everything" needed no change (it buys up to what blueprints reached, key or none; abs mode maxes everything). B. ECU UP ON PURCHASE: the pack reveal's bar (render/blueprintcard.js progressBar) no longer glows for an ECU reached: it glows only for UNLOCKED! / KEY: UNLOCKED!; a reached-not-bought ECU writes a quiet 'Upgrade ready - ECU n' (or '- MAX ECU') under the bar, the new pip outlined, count word READY. The glow and the cinematic are now `render/ecuupgrade.js upMoment` on a successful `Ecu.buy`: the bought pip glows (`.sr-pips i.fresh`), an 'ECU UP - ECU3 bought' flag (green; gold 'MAX ECU 5 bought' for the last step) in the full view, and render/ecucine.js's ECU UP cinematic (ECU MAX for the last step) plays over the screen through a lazy `import('./unlockcine.js')` (skipped with reduced motion, `&nocine`, or no WebGL); `Ecu.buy` dispatches `ecuchange` with `{ car, from, to, max }`. NOTE: before this the cinematic was only reachable by address (`?unlockcine=ecu:<id>`), nothing in the game played it; now a purchase does. C. BARS: one pure rule, `src/delivery/render/barfill.js barFill({have,toNext,ecu,bought,max}) -> { pct, over, full }` (render side, outside the replay hash): reached above bought = 100% and `over`; the last step reached = full (over while unbought). Used by: the pack reveal bar (blueprintcard.js; `revealProgress` states now carry `bought` and `over`), Jim's stall (render/jimshop.js; `jimshop.js stand()` carries `bought`/`over`; line 'Upgrade ready - ECU n', gain text 'Unlocks the ECU n upgrade!' instead of 'Reaches ECU n!', no preview when over), the showroom card and the full view (render/showroom.js blueprintBar; words READY / 'Upgrade to ECUn ready to buy'; `carCard().over`), and the pips (outlined = unlocked, not bought, as before). Overfill look (existing bar styles, no new component): the track's overflow opens, the fill becomes running gold stripes, a glow spills past the end (`.over` in blueprintcard.css, jimshop.css, showroom.css). The car sources screen (render/carsources.js) has no bar (checked); the old lot (garage.js) was left alone (E). D. BLUEPRINT CAP: `Ecu.cap(car)` = X x steps (blueprint car: max x X; key car: (max - 1) x X; 0 for a car with no ECU, an unknown id, a special or 1-gold key car; a 1-ECU BLUEPRINT car, the Bubble Car, keeps 5: it needs X to be unlocked); `Ecu.addBlueprints` clamps to it (a saved count above it is never reduced, and a maxed-flag car takes none); `Ecu.counted / capped / room`. Reads clamp, data stays: showroomData bp, jimshop `stand`/`needed`. Packs (packs.js) [the REGULAR-pool half of this was UNDONE on 15-Oct by the owner, item 321: the rule is the featured cards' only]: `draw` takes capped cars out of the REGULAR pools (`dealable`: a rarity with none left leaves the odds and the others' shares grow; only if EVERY blueprint car is capped is the whole pool kept, so a pack is never empty and the blueprints are simply not added, the cards shown 'maxed'), asks the maxed state once per car per pack (one snapshot, as before), re-deals a slot whose car has no room left once the pack's earlier slots are counted (same rarity first, then any); the FEATURED slots already left out an exhausted entry and are unchanged (`maxedNow` = capped); `Packs.open` clamps through `addBlueprints` and sets `card.given` (what the save took), `card.cut` (the stack as dealt, when the cap cut it) and `card.n` = given; the reveal and its bars use `given`; the 'What's inside' sheet lists a capped car 'maxed', chance 0. CHANGE OF AN OLD RULE: the 12-Oct rule 'the regular slots may still give a maxed car's blueprint as a duplicate' is gone (the owner's cap). Jim's stall still lists a capped car but as 'Maxed out' (disabled; his rotation is seeded and must not move when the save does); `needed()` now reads the cap. Carsources: 'It has every blueprint it needs for its max ECU' and the Concept Strike key text says the max ECU is BOUGHT. E. garage.js: untouched. F. Decisions recorded above. HASH (checked with vite.config.js logicFiles()): IN the replay hash and changed: `ecu.js` (accepted), `showroomData.js`, `carsources.js`; `progress.js` and `config.js` NOT touched (no new save field; the cookie is unchanged, +0 bytes). - [x] 294. THE ORBIT CAMERA for replays and the end of a run (the owner, 15-Oct: "a rotating camera for replays", then: "seamlessly allow the player to orbit the camera from the gameplay cam, and a button to lock it back. Orbit while the replay is playing"). Branch `claude/replay-orbit`, not merged, no version bump. All render-only: `render/orbit.js` (new), `render/orbitdemo.js` (new: a test harness, loaded only by `?orbitdemo=`), and small touches in `render/ghostcar.js`, `render/racewatch.js` (`firstSolid`, `prepareSolids`), `render/road.js` (one flag on the terrain mesh), `render/cargo.js` (the orbit's taps and drags, and O, are not a skip of the delivery), `render/hud.js`, `main.js` (the camera dispatch), `delivery/index.html`, `style.css`, URL-PARAMS.md. NOT `config.js` nor any file in the replay fingerprint (`logicFiles()` run: 66 files, none of the changed ones; the orbit's numbers are in orbit.js, not CONFIG.photo). HOW IT WORKS. IN A REPLAY (playing at 1x/2x/4x, paused or held at its end) a DRAG (mouse button or one finger, past 6 px) on the game view takes hold of the chase camera where it is drawn that frame: yaw, pitch and radius are worked out from the camera's place relative to the car (radius clamped to 15..40 m, pitch to 5..60 degrees) and its aim is kept at where the chase camera looked and eased onto the car over 0.5 s, so the first frame is exactly the chase frame; then the drag turns it. A plain tap or click, a wobble under 6 px, and a wheel or a pinch on the chase camera do nothing. The replay goes on playing while it is orbited (no pause), and a drag-started orbit never turns by itself and keeps the HUD. A small panel bottom right (40 px buttons): Chase view (eases the camera from the orbit pose back to the live chase camera over 0.5 s, position and aim blended, then a new drag starts a new orbit) and Ghost: on/off (default OFF: the best run on the level and side, by the replay's clock, never the very run being watched). The Orbit button in the row (Pause, 1x, Orbit, Exit level) and O start the other kind: it turns by itself (0.22 rad/s) until the player takes hold, again after 4 s alone, with the HUD hidden as photo mode hides it. AT THE END OF A LIVE RUN (the delivery at the kerb, the results): no drag-to-enter (it would fight the tap that skips the delivery); Orbit alone in the row at the top, and Look around on the results (hides them; Results in the panel brings them back). Wheel or pinch zooms inside 15..40 m while orbiting. THE NUDGE: a ray from the car to the camera and two 1.6 m to either side (racewatch `firstSolid`; the terrain by `landAt`: a ray at its mesh took ~40 ms); where blocked the camera is raised, then drawn in; closer than 5 m the usual camera is used that frame. The scenery is boxed once as the orbit comes on offer so a look costs under ~1.5 ms every 0.1 s (headless). Left-hand levels go through `aim()`; Photo mode is untouched. SEEN, 15-Oct, in a REAL REPLAY driven headless (`?orbitdemo=` records a ghost-driven run A to its delivery, a slower run B, then plays B back with Replay.watch; stills with scripts/shots.mjs, /tmp/replaycam-investigation/o2/): the chase view mid-replay with the REPLAY badge and the row Pause / 1x / Orbit / Exit level; a synthetic mouse drag entering the orbit: the pose in the car's own terms (m ahead, m to the side, m up, look direction, fov) on the last chase frame was [-14, 0.61, 9.9, 0.923, 0, -0.385, 76.798] and on the first orbit frame [-14, 0.61, 9.9, 0.923, 0, -0.385, 76.798] (the same to three decimals; six steps later); the drag then swung it 88.7 degrees round (yaw 176.9 to 88.2) while the replay clock went on (steps 908 to 926, 7.57 s to 7.72 s, not paused), auto-spin off; the way back held at 0.15, 0.5 and 0.85 of the blend (the camera pose moved [0.5, 14.0, 9.9] to [-1.7, 11.9] to [-6.8, 7.1] to [-11.8, 2.4] to the chase [-14, 0.2, 9.9], the look direction ahead 0 to 0.20 to 0.63 to 0.89 to 0.92, stills lock_015 / lock_05 / lock_085); a tap, a wobble and a wheel on the chase camera did nothing; the Ghost button in the panel turning the replay ghost on (the line under the clock saying 'Ghost 46 m ahead', now put under the REPLAY badge instead of behind it); a 390x844 phone still of the replay orbited (HUD whole, top row Pause / 1x / Exit level, the panel bottom right). Earlier, live-run stills: Tokyo, Stelvio, Spa behind / beside / in front, Spa's grandstand before and after the nudge, the delivery and results with Orbit / Look around. NOT VERIFIED: a replay played and orbited by hand with real input, touch drag and pinch on a real phone, Mi Pad frame times, the orbit in a tunnel or on a left-hand level, 2x/4x speed, a replay held at its end, and the pale blue replay ghost car itself could not be told apart in the still (only its button and gap line). The harness: the page's virtual clock gives a script only a handful of frames, so the swing is dispatched in one go after the first frame. Checks: node --check, eslint on the changed files, `delivery-tests.mjs --group replay` and `--group render` (--no-lock), the vite build.   - UFO CHECK (merge finding, 15-Oct): `.ufo-check.mjs` failed on this branch ("some are smoking within a second (0)") and passed on main. Not a game change: the check seeds Math.random BEFORE `boot()`, and Vite's module runner draws on Math.random once a module as it loads the game (nanoid), so the branch's three new render modules ate 63 more draws and every later game draw moved (other traffic under the saucer). Proof: re-seeded after the boot, main and the branch give identical runs on seeds 2026, 7 and 99 (90/48/4, 86/50/1, 88/46/0 cars wrecked / spun / smoking at 1 s). Fix: the check re-seeds after `boot()` (no criterion weakened). Note for the owner: "smoking within a second" is incidental: a healthy car loses about 9 health in the first second of the burn (7 e^(0.5 t)), never to 75%; the ones that smoke by 1 s were hurt in spin-out crashes, so seed 99 gives 0 on main too. Re-run on both main and this branch: gimmicks group (only the known `.pursuit-check` failure, on both), `.mysteries-check`, `.hud-check` (pass on both).

Not a lane closure (Lanes closing is cones and a taper that merges everyone): a dead-end lane is OPEN and inviting, with cash in it, and ENDS. A level's `deadEnds: [{ s, lane, kind: 'shutter' | 'wall', length? }]` (levels.js, levelSchema.js; `CONFIG.deadEnd`: length 120, sign 100, warn 120, stripes 24). lanegimmicks.js puts an obstacle of kind `laneEnd` at s across the lane (a lane wide, 2.8 m tall; obstacleKinds.laneEnd costs nothing: it is never an ordinary knock); Collision.hitObstacles checks it FIRST, before any protection is honoured. WALL (nothing behind it, a block of structure over the lane): a SUPER INSTAKILL: a plain car, a Ghost, a tank, armour, a fresh drop's shield (Player.shield) and an invincible mystery (damageScale is never asked) all die. - [x] 306. SHOWROOM: THE CAR FLICKERED ON EACH SWITCH (the owner, 15-Oct, Xiaomi Pad 7: "flickers each time I switch a car in the showroom model view"). Branch `claude/showroom-switch-flicker`, NOT merged, no version bump. TRACED (render/showroom.js `goTo` -> `pick` -> `showDetail`, render/showroomview.js `show`): the whole detail is rebuilt (`detail.replaceChildren`, new `.sr-dpic`); `ShowroomView.show()` drops the old model, builds the new one, moves the one 2D canvas into the new picture, and the first draw was left to the NEXT animation frame, drawn at fade alpha 0 (`bounceOffset` alpha 0 at t=0, 0.25 s fade-in). REPRODUCED (headless Chromium over the DevTools protocol, touch emulation, 1280x800 at ratio 3, ArrowRight x3, a per-frame log of the live canvas's alpha): the first painted frame after every switch had the NEW words and an EMPTY canvas (alpha sum 0), then the car faded up over the next frames (software GL, so stalls of 100 to 630 ms stretched it): old car gone in one frame, blank stage, new car fading in = a blink. CAUSE (certain for the blank frame; that it is THE flicker the owner sees on the tablet is likely, not seen on it): the first frame's shader compile and texture upload ran in a frame whose result is painted blank. FIX (render-only, replays unaffected): on a switch (another car after one was on screen) `show()` draws the new car's first frame at once, in the same task as the new words (so the compile stall happens before the browser paints and the old picture stays up until the new car is in the canvas), and that switch skips the alpha fade (the car still drops and bounces); the first opening of the showroom and reduced motion keep the fade; `draw` split into `draw`/`frameAt`, dt kept non-negative. CHECKED: the same probe after the fix: no empty frame at any switch (alpha sum never 0); node --check, eslint, `--group render --no-lock` (12 scripts ok). NOT verified: on the Mi Pad itself (144 Hz, real GPU, ratio 3); the corner key (a key car) and the small card still (`stillCar`, filled one animation frame after the words) were not measured and may still pop in one frame later (suspected, minor); the phone layout's slide animation (sr-from-next, phone only) untouched.

THE TAB: `delivery/modeleditor.html?car=<id>&tab=place&slot=roof|cargo|wing|plate` ("Places"; `render/placementedit.js`, render-only, imported by the editor only): Roof / Cargo / Wing / Plates; each prop slot shows a real prop from the registry (default GT wing, roof box, crate stack; any of the 34) inside its canonical box or cylinder, the car's own tagged props of an open slot hidden as the game hides them (a locked slot keeps its own, e.g. the LMP's spoiler); the plates as white rectangles (Rear / Front, On / Off). Edit by a tap on the car (the slot goes there, snapped), dragging the shape over the surface, the view's Move / Turn / Size handles (bigger on a touch screen), or numbers (X Y Z, Turn, Scale, Inset cm; a plate's Y Z Width Tilt); "Centred (X 0)" on by default; "Box + cyl together" (the other shape follows: same scale, base, inset); a new scale keeps the base standing; Snap (onto the car's own body under it, the least inset that makes the rim touch), Fit max (the generator's own `feasible` search at that spot, then up to 30 cm either way; no cap, the floor kept), Auto for a plate (the plate search), Lock / Unlock with a reason, Reset to generated. CHIPS (the audit's measures, live): fits, floats, rim off, inset, clips N cm (what), on glass / lamp / see-through / body kit, % over air, hits <other slot>, a wing's width against the surface it stands on, the scale; a plate's flat / uneven mm, floats / sunk, tilt off, on glass / lamp / wheel, hidden, width. SAVING IS THE BUILD: "Write to build" (dev server only: `vite.config.js` plugin, POST `/__placements`, same-origin, no page reload for its own write) writes the car's HAND ROW into its `render/propanchors/slice-<n>.json` (the generator's own row shape, `lim: 'hand'`, `by: 'editor'`, `prev`: the row it replaced with `src` generated | hand | editor) and a plate into `render/plateanchors.js` HAND (`{ r, f, by: 'editor', prev }` on its own line, a model's id taken out of HAND_NONE if needed); "Diff" shows the file diff before writing, "Undo write" puts `prev` back. With no dev server (the deployed page, a phone): Download / Copy the change file, then `node scripts/bake-placements.mjs <file> [--dry]` (the same code: `scripts/placements-lib.mjs`). A draft lives only in the page and `localStorage['delivery.modeleditor.placementDrafts']` (the editor's; the game never reads it). PROTECTED: the generator (`scripts/prop-anchors.mjs`, now writing through `placements-lib.mjs`) rewrites only `cars` and prints the editor rows it kept; `.propanchors-check` lists editor rows as the owner's call and does not test them for standing on the surface. Rim = one merged geometry per design (backing dish, lip, spokes or blades, cap; vertex-shaded, the set's hub colour), a CHILD OF EACH TYRE, one mesh per outer face (a centre wheel both), shared geometry and material, tagged `wheelRing` + material `wheelHub`, 12 mm off the tyre's face (or its tread blocks); the stock hub is hidden under it (shown again when the rim goes). Triangles a face: 5-spoke 160, twin 220, turbine 220, blade spinner 144, twin spinner 176 (a 4-wheeler +576 to +880; 2 to 8 extra draw calls, +1 a face while the blur disc shows). TURNING: speed rims (5-spoke, twin, turbine) turn by distance rolled over the wheel's radius: render/items.js gives the player's model `userData.roll` from the change in Player.s over Track.pathScale (so pause, replay speed and reverse follow), clamped to 0.45 of the spoke gap a frame, and a translucent disc fades in over the spokes in three steps from 0.18 to 0.4 of the gap a frame (commuter at 60 fps: step 1 at ~4 m/s, full at ~8 m/s); on show (garage, showroom, Cosmetics) they idle at 1.2 rad/s; spinners turn at 6 rad/s everywhere, stopped or not. Per frame: one quaternion per rim mesh, no allocation (from the model's `animate`, wrapped by dressWheels). The four clock-driven models (models.js f1 + the two at 219 / 333, ideaModels3 Monowheel) now turn their wheels by `roll` as the player's car, a gentle 3 rad/s on show (`userData.onShow`, set by showcar.js; was 30), and as before (30 rad/s) as traffic; a rim on their self-turning tyres takes the tyre's turn off its own (`userData.rollsWheels`). NOT SEEN: motion (stills only; turning verified numerically), a real Mi Pad (frame cost, draw calls), night / headlights, dents with rims, the Evil paint, every car from every side (the 12 above only), the menu card, cinematics (rims show there, idling).
  - 310 FOLLOW-UP (the owner's decisions on the report, 15-Oct; same branch). (2, 5) Old sets stay rim-free; circuswagon, quadturbo, dodgem stay skipped. (3) BLOCKED WHEELS, "depends how blocked": the all-or-nothing ray rule is replaced (render/rims.js roomFor). Per face, 36 rays along the axle measure `clear` (share meeting nothing) and `depth` (how far off the tyre the nearest bodywork is). The rim goes BEHIND what covers it, as a real wheel sits behind its arch: WHOLE if it fits (depth >= 61.5 mm), else a FLAT variant of the same design (no dish, lip and spokes side by side in one plane, spokes' backs sunk into the tyre, cap 4.5 mm over: 9.5 mm deep, 5 mm off the tyre, needs depth >= 19 mm; a design's sidewall ring gives way on that face). A face wears a rim if clear >= 0.9 (whole; a mudguard edge or bracket over a sliver is let be) or clear >= 0.4 and it fits behind; else stock. Inner twin tyres (face against the outer tyre) are left out. Then PER AXLE (tyres within r/3 along the car): the worse face decides, so an axle never mixes rim and stock, unless lopsided (one face >= 0.97 open, the other < 0.05), and an axle is all whole or all flat. Thresholds tuned by looking (the owner's 0.6-0.7 left every half-mudguarded classic stock; at 0.4 + fit-behind they look right). Result: 190 of 220 cars wear rims (was 182); no rims at all: bug (bodywork 1-8 mm off the wheels), sidecar (5-15 mm), ufo, earned-ufo; mixed by axle: tuk-tuk (front centre wheel stock, 15 mm), snake / super-snake (rear stock, 8 mm), tinsnail (rear stock, 2 mm), swancar (rear stock), rallywedge (rear stock, 7 mm). Flat rims under mudguards: bubble, keiroadster, teardrop, flyingcar, dragster (middle), solarracer, tipper / mixer / wrecker fronts, dunebuggy, armoured, bathtub, gyrocar, mower, duckwagon, cabledrum, gameviewer. SEEN (/tmp/rims-build/border3-front34.png, border3-right34.png, before: border-*.png): the half-covered wheels read as rims behind the arches; no rim over a spat. Honest misses: the snake's and tinsnail's rear and the tuk-tuk's front show the stock hub beside rimmed wheels on the other axle; the bug's lower wheel halves show stock hubs. (4) CINEMATICS: one opt-out, `makeShowCar(car, { stock: true })` -> render/showcar.js `dressCosmetics(model, car, { stock })`, the ONE hook for every cosmetic (wheel set + rim, plate, props; render/items.js playerModel now goes through it too). - [x] 315. ROADWORKS DIVERSIONS, a new gimmick (the owner, 15-Oct: "parts of the road to have cones across, as road works where lanes temporarily change sides"; branch `claude/roadworks` from main v1.1.140, NOT merged, version not bumped; placeholder number). THE LATERAL-OFFSET LAYER (track.js, chosen over a lane-index remap and over a world-space bend like a split, because everything that follows a lane already asks Track where the lane is): a level's `diversions: [{ from, to, kind, by?, limit? }]` (levels.js header, FIELDS in levelSchema.js, `CONFIG.diversion`) gives each side of the road, per s, `ci` lanes closed from the inside, `co` from the outside and `d` m of sideways move, each smoothstep-eased (`Track.divert`); laneOffset, openLane (a lane counts as closed from its first cone), nearestLane, edge, laneLo / laneHi, lanesOn and the pavement (lo / hi: never narrower, wider where lanes move out) read it, so the player's lane assist, traffic, pickups and obstacles placed on a lane, rivals, the camera and the editor's map follow the temporary lanes with no code of their own. What the lanes leave is a WORKS ZONE (`Track.works`: between the two ways, or outside a side whose kerb lanes close): `Track.bounds` gives the free band a car is in and physics.js `keepOnRoad` holds EVERY car at its line as at the road's edge (player.js's edge-brake too: it straightens up, never stops). The player is CARRIED by a lane's sideways move as by a bend (`Track.carry`: a shift needs no steering); a lane closing is not carried: left in it, the taper's line squeezes the car over and it knocks the TAPER CONES (roadworks.js: ordinary `cone` obstacles, a cone's box and cost, 0.35 m into the zone on a line coming across a lane, 0.5 m where it draws back, so a squeezed car clips them and a car in its lane never does): the counterplay is the signs, the arrow board and moving over early. Drawn (render/roadworks.js, render-only): continuous YELLOW temporary markings along the lanes where they are now (buildStrip / buildDashes on Track's lane functions: no staircases), the road's white lines cut there (road.js); red and white water-filled barrier blocks along every zone line that runs straight on (instanced, two meshes), a row ACROSS the closed lane at each taper's end with a LIT ARROW BOARD behind it (flashing, its lamps a 128 x 96 canvas), light towers in the wide zones, the closed road a darker planed patch, the signs on the verge (ROAD WORKS, the lanes, the limit, END; 128 x 128 canvases), plant (roller, excavator, dump truck) parked 4 m and more off the pavement, beacons flashing. Props only: no figures. Gimmicks card "Roadworks diversions" (The road itself, amber), words in gimmickText.json, messages "Roadworks ahead: lanes shift." etc. Replays and ghosts: deterministic logic, a ghost car replays what the player did; THE REPLAY FINGERPRINT MOVES (track.js, physics.js, player.js, traffic.js, config.js, collision.js, levels.js and levelSchema.js are logic): accepted. True speed: pathScale uses lat, which is the lane's true offset, so a moved lane on a bend is scaled right; the S itself adds under 0.5% of length, ignored as lane changes are. No minimap in the HUD; the road card lists the card.

TRAFFIC READS THE DIVERSIONS (the owner: "Make sure traffic reacts to it"; same branch). traffic.js: within CONFIG.diversion.look m of a diversion a car keeps its lane (no lane change for its own reasons, no feud: think() and the rival chase are off; any other move is allowed only into a lane open all the way through and with room: a jam's impatience, a hunt, a block, a racer's overtake); it steers with its lane's own sideways drift added (`laneDrift`: it follows a taper's line through the cones instead of lagging over them; long vehicles move across at 2.5 m/s at most; no wander); it eases off to the works' limit from `slow` m before (each driver its own 0.94 to 1.06 of it, an evil one 8% more, a racer 25% more), brake lights as it does; queues brake firmly nose to tail (`worksFollow`: the speed that still stops 3 m short of what is ahead); a car whose lane closes ahead MERGES when there is room in the open lane (`zipRoom`: clear ahead and behind at the speeds they go; a courteous driver as soon as it can, the rest within 90 m of the cones) or waits short of the first cone, signalling, and a courteous driver in the open lane lets one in (holds back 5 m behind it): a ZIP; cars are not put on the road in a lane that closes within 150 m; an ambulance in a diversion follows at 1.2 x the limit and never arrests or busts (no room to pull over). Rivals are traffic cars (the same rules); a ghost car replays the player. - [x] 317. THE THREE VARIANTS (same branch). (A) `merge`: the player's kerb lane closes through a 40 m taper (traffic zips into the lane beside; the works are the kerb lane and the shoulder); (B) `shift`, the main one: the player's lanes move `by` m (CONFIG.diversion.by, 4) out onto a widened shoulder over a 60 m taper (`shiftTaper`: lanes with traffic in them move more gently than a closing one, so a 16 m semi follows it), the works between the two ways, and back; (C) `cross`, a CONTRAFLOW: the oncoming side's inner lane closes over 40 m (oncoming traffic zips the other way), 30 m on the player's two lanes cross over into it (2.3 m) with 1.2 m of water-filled barrier between them and the oncoming lane, the works on the player's kerb side, and back. FAIRNESS of C: the barrier strip is a works zone, a line no car crosses, so a head-on through it cannot happen; the oncoming lane is beside, readable, behind barriers; the player keeps two lanes to pass in; signs CONTRAFLOW with arrows each way and the arrow boards. Level problems reported (track.js): too short, a merge on one lane, a crossover without two oncoming lanes, by and limit out of range, near an exit, bridge, narrowing, tunnel, split, stop / go, reversible lane, fresh tarmac, boost pad, dead end, junction or another diversion.

ROADWORKS, a hidden showcase level (the owner: "A roadworks level would be a good showcase"; same branch). `?hidden=roadworks` (HIDDEN_LEVELS, so Options > Hidden levels lists it; banks nothing; not in THEME_LEVELS or INSERTED_AT). DISTRICTS of 280-560 m along the road, picked by how central the stretch is (one middle at 56% of the level, two on a level over 4.2 km), never the same twice running: the edge of town (houses, two storeys or one, gable roofs, a garden wall, a tree; low blocks of flats), industrial (warehouses in corrugated metal with loading doors, sawtooth roofs glazed on their upright faces or flat roofs with vents, a two-storey office at the front, a palisade along the yard), the high street (brick and rendered terraces of 2-5 storeys over shops, stone sills and lintels, cornices, chimneys, awnings), midtown (flats with balconies, banded mid-rise with a glass top floor set back, shops under them), downtown (glass curtain-wall and banded towers on a podium of shops or a lobby, one to three setbacks, plant rooms, masts with a red light), little plazas between. Behind the street a BACKDROP of towers 48-150 m back, taller downtown, lower and rendered or brick at the edges (none on the Ring Road, which has its own). THE FACADES are the shader's (one material for every box: windows in whole bays, a ground floor of shops (window, door, a fascia in one of eight colours with abstract light bars for its sign, no lettering), a lobby, or loading doors; curtain walls with mullions and spandrels reflecting the sky's colour; ribbon windows; at night a share of the windows lit per building and per floor (warm, cool, the odd blue), shops and fascias glowing, lamp heads and adverts glowing). NIGHT: no real lights: under each lamp an additive cone and a pool of light, faded by vertex colour (no hard edge). KEPT CLEAR (city.js clearings): nothing at all within 30 m of a bridge, 25 m of a tunnel's ends, 18 m of a level crossing, 50 m of a drawbridge, 20 m of a ford; no furniture by a target, a camera, a low bridge, a shade stretch, site works, stop / go, a school crossing, an exit's fork and merge noses (both roads), the Ring Road's set pieces (pavement and lamps kept there); in water stages only the lamps; for Roadworks' `diversions` (forward-compatible) no furniture from 220 m before to 60 m after, and no building where its plant parks 5.5 m off. Every box is one instance of one mesh: boxes in a mesh a 600 m of road and crowns a 1,800 m (out of sight: culled), the roofs' wedges (sawtooth and gable) one mesh, the lamps' light one (night), the pavements and kerbs one merged mesh with vertex colours. ONE canvas texture drawn in code, 256 x 256 RGBA (256 KB, about 341 KB with mipmaps): red brick courses, green mottle, blue corrugation.
